Maybe use sequences corresponding to finite projective plane / difference sets. Let's explore binary difference sets. For a binary sequence length n, weight w. Full convolution c_k counts ordered pairs with sum k. If sequence is symmetric? We can choose support S subset of {0,...,n-1}. c_k = |S ∩ (k-S)| (ordered? Actually number of pairs (i,j)∈S^2 with i+j=k). We want max c_k small. Sum c_k = w^2. There are 2n-1 lags. Lower bound max ≥ w^2/(2n-1). For binary, to get F small: F=2n max/w^2 ≥ 2n/(2n-1)≈1. So possible if supports form a "perfect difference set" in sums (not differences). This is like a set with all sums equally distributed—Sidon/set with interval sums. For S = arithmetic progression, sums counts triangular max w. For S = modular difference set, sums maybe? Note sum constraint is finite interval, not cyclic. But if we take S as quadratic residues modulo p and map to [0,p-1], the number of representations as sum maybe approximately? For subset of [0,n-1], sum counts relate to additive energy. There exist sets with low additive energy? Yes, Sidon sets have pair sums mostly distinct, so max ≤1 for 6n-? But a Sidon set of size w in [n] requires n ~ w^2. Then max c small. For Sidon, sum c_k ≈w^2, number lags 2n, max maybe O(1) if Sidon? Then F ~ 2n*O(1)/w^2. If w ~√n, F ~ O(√n), not good. Need w ~n to get F~1 but then cannot be Sidon; large subsets of interval have additive patterns.

Not same as shift. However if we take cyclic and unwrap? Let's express c_k = ∑_{i+j=k} a_i a_j. This is not shift autocorrelation ∑ a_i a_{i+k}. Maybe we can leverage known optimal "linear programming relaxation" from hint: maximize sum b s.t. conv(a,b)≤M. The iteration. We can implement that! It was SOTA used to approach? The previous program did block perturbation and got 2. The hint describes an algorithm to reduce max convolution. We can implement a version of the ascent/iteration:
- Start with a (nonnegative, sum maybe sqrt(2n) but scale invariant).
- M = max(conv(a,a)).
- Solve LP: maximize sum b subject to b≥0 and conv(a,b)≤M. Since a fixed, conv(a,b) are linear in b, objective linear. This is a linear program! Variables b_j length n (valid lags? The LP in hint uses b length n, conv(f*g) length 2n-1 constraints ≤M, b_j≥0). Maximize sum b. This LP can be solved with cvxpy? Allowed solvers. Then rescale b to sum sqrt(2n), update a←(1-t)a+t b. Repeat. This reduces max conv? The hint says yes. This is a sophisticated method! We can implement with scipy linprog or cvxpy. Variables n, constraints 2n-1 * b <= M. For n up to maybe several hundred, LP size manageable. This could find good sequences.

Let's understand LP: Given f nonnegative, M=max f*f. Maximize ∑ b_j subject to b≥0, (f*b) *(?) Wait "||f*g_0||∞≤M" means convolution of f and g_0 (two sequences) bounded by M. Since f fixed, constraints linear in g: ∑ f_i g_{t-i} ≤ M for all t. Maximize sum g. This LP solution g tends to put mass in positions where f is low relative to constraints? Then rescale g to normalization and mix. This is like finding a direction with low cross-correlation with f. It might escape all-ones.

We can implement this iteration. Need choose normalization. Objective scale invariant; set S=1 for convenience. But LP bound M = max f*f. If S=1. Then solve max sum g s.t. f*g≤M, g≥0. The optimum may have large sum? Then scale g to S=1: g_norm = g / sum_g. Update a = (1-t)a + t g_norm. Since a,g sum1, new sum1. The claim: iteration produces nonincreasing ||f*f||∞ and objective? For normalized sum1, objective=2n M. If M nonincreasing, F nonincreasing. Great.

Let's detail LP iteration:
- Let a length n nonnegative, sum S=√(2n) perhaps. M=max conv(a,a).
- LP: maximize ∑ b_j s.t. b_j≥0, A b ≤ M, where A_{t,j}=a_{t-j} if valid.
- If optimum sum_b >0, normalize b' = b / sum_b * √(2n). Then a_new=(1-t)a + t b'. Choose t small e.g., 0.1 or based on line search: try t values to minimize exact objective. Since nonincreasing claim, t small. Repeat.

Let's design code:
```
import numpy as np, time, sys
import scipy.optimize as scipy_opt

EPS = 1e-12
DEFAULT_SEED = 1

def norm_sum(a):
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15: return None
    return a/s

def fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    S=a.sum()
    if S < 0.01: return np.inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,None)
    S=np.sum(a)
    if S < 0.01: return np.inf
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    M=np.max(c)
    return 2.0*len(a)*M/(S*S)
```
But for normalized a sum1, no clip.

Build A for LP:
```
def build_convolution_matrix(a):
    n=len(a)
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    for t in range(2*n-1):
        lo=max(0,t-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,t)
        if lo<=hi:
            idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
            A[t, idx] = a[t - idx]
    return A
```
could be O(n^2). For n=2000, 4M allocations per A; ok but repeated iterations maybe heavy. We can build sparse? Use dense. For n=1000, 2M entries, 16MB; okay. Rebuild each iteration because a changes. 100 iterations -> heavy but okay maybe. Could optimize using banded sparse rows with scipy.sparse.lil? But linprog with dense might be faster for small. We can restrict n ≤300 for LP to keep fast.

Line search update:
```
def update_with_direction(a,b,t):
    a_new=(1-t)*a + t*b
    a_new=np.clip(a_new,0,None)
    return norm_sum(a_new)
```
Choose t: Use `for t in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]` maybe. Evaluate fast_score. If best improves current, update. If no improve, try smaller.

LP solve function:
```
def lp_direction(a, seed, max_time?):
    n=len(a); M=fast_max_conv(a)
    if not finite or M<=0: return None
    A=build_convolution_matrix(a)
    b_ub=M
    # maybe scale A and b by max(A) to improve?
    c_base=-np.ones(n)
    c = c_base + 1e-5*np.random.rand(n) # in minimization? This makes some variables less preferred? c=-1+eps*r, variables with larger r higher cost, so avoid; okay.
    res=scipy_opt.linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=(0,None), method='highs')
    if res.success: b=res.x; b=np.clip(b,0,None)
    else: b=None
    s=b.sum()
    if s>1e-12: return b/s
    return None
```
But c=-1+eps*r: since all primary -1, secondary r penalizes variables; if eps too large may reduce sum. Use eps=1e-3? For uniform n large, sum constraint dominance? Maybe. We can also not perturb and rely on HiGHS variable selection. Add small random to break ties; eps=1e-6.

LP may be infeasible due numerical if M slightly below; use b_ub=M*(1+1e-9)+1e-12.

Run iterations:
```
def lp_iteration(a, seed, max_iters, time_limit):
   n=len(a); rng=default_rng(seed)
   best=norm_sum(a); best_score=fast_score(best)
   for iters:
      for _ in range(nlp):
         b=lp_direction(best,rng)
         if b is None: continue
         # line search
         current=best_score
         best_t=None
         for t in [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5]:
             cand=(1-t)*best + t*b; cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); cand=norm_sum(cand)
             sc=fast_score(cand)
             if sc+1e-12 < current: current=sc; best_t=cand
         if best_t is not None:
             best=best_t; best_score=current
         else:
             # maybe larger or direction alone
             cand=norm_sum(b); sc=fast_score(cand); if sc<best_score: best=cand
      if no improvement for some iters break
      if time > limit break
   return best,best_score
```

Let's design a robust multi-stage solver:

Stage 0: Evaluate provided height_sequence_1 if valid, normalize? Keep best.

Stage 1: Small-n exact/heuristic optimization (n=2..12):
- For each n, create random starts and two-block/ramp starts.

Implement objective:
```
def smooth_objective(x, p=10):
   a=np.asarray(x,float)
   S=a.sum()
   if S<1e-12: return 1e6
   a=a/S
   c=np.convolve(a,a)
   # c nonnegative
   M=(np.sum(c**p)**(1/p))*? If p large, M approx max but less than max? Average power less than max; minimizing may not minimize max. Could use logsumexp: M = (1/p)*logsumexp(p*c) approximate? Since scale? We can use soft max: M = max_c + (1/p)*log(∑exp(p(c-max))) to be close and smooth. But gradient numerical. Use p=20, c≤1, exp safe (e^20 ~4e8). c**p safe.
   return 2*n*M  # sum=1
```
But if we normalize inside, variables can be any; bounds [0,1]. Better fix sum by penalty? L-BFGS-B with bounds no equality; objective invariant to scale, so nonconvex flat scaling. Could normalize inside, but gradient along scale zero; okay. But maximize use bounds; start normalized.

Maybe use `method='SLSQP'` with equality sum? For n small. But many vars.

Let's consider implementing a pure Python local search with "break active lags":
- Given a normalized, find max lag m. We want reduce c_m. c_m = ∑ a_i a_{m-i}. If we decrease variables that contribute heavily to c_m and increase variables that don't (or contribute to lower lags), greedily. For each i, derivative of c_m: 2 a_{m-i} (if valid) (ordered). Moving mass from i to j changes c_m by 2ε(a_{m-j}-a_{m-i})? We can perform a quick coordinate move: pick i with high a_{m-i}, j with low a_{m-j}, transfer small amount. This is like steepest descent for max. Repeat. Could be efficient.

Algorithm:
```
def reduce_max(a, max_lag):
  n=len(a); c=np.convolve(a,a); maxes positions where c close max.
  For candidate max lag k, define weight w_i = a_{k-i} if valid else 0. We want decrease ∑ a_i w_i. Move mass from i with high a_i*w_i to j with low a_j*w_j, but also avoid increasing other lags.
```
This is similar to LP subgradient. We can sample moves:
- Normalize. For each max lag k, compute W_i = (a_{k-i} if 0≤k-i<n else 0). The contribution to c_k is ∑ a_i W_i (but double count? c_k =∑ a_i a_{k-i}; if we treat gradient, c_k = a dot W; gradient wrt a_i = W_i). Move δ from p to q: change c_k = δ(W_q - W_p). Choose p high W_p a_p? Actually to reduce c_k, move mass from high W_p to low W_q. But need ensure q's own square not too high etc. We can try random pairs p,q and small transfer, normalize? Transfer keeps sum. Evaluate score; accept if improve. This is a coordinate gradient local search, cheap.

Implement `coordinate_improve(a, rng, iters)`:
```
for _ in range(iters):
    c=np.convolve(a,a); maxpos = indices where c >= M*(1-1e-3)
    k=random choice maxpos
    n=len(a); W=np.zeros(n)
    for i in range(n): j=k-i; if 0<=j<n: W[i]=a[j]
    # choose p weighted by a_i*W_i, q where W_q low
    p_prob = np.maximum(a*W, 0); q_prob = 1/(1+W) maybe
    p = weighted choice, q = weighted choice
    delta = 0.05 * a[p] maybe
    if p==q continue
    b=a.copy(); b[p]-=delta; b[q]+=delta; clip>=0; normalize? transfer keeps sum; if p underflow, okay.
    if score < best: accept
```

Coordinate transfer local improve:
```
def coordinate_transfer_improve(a, rng, max_steps=100):
   n=len(a); best=a; best_sc=fast_score(a)
   for _ in range(max_steps):
      c=np.convolve(best,best); M=c.max()
      if not finite: break
      idxs=np.where(c >= M*(1-1e-3))[0]
      k=int(rng.choice(idxs))
      W=np.zeros(n)
      lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
      # W[i]=best[k-i]
      for i in range(lo,hi+1): W[i]=best[k-i]
      # choose p with high contribution a_i W_i, q with low W_q
      p_weights=np.maximum(best*W,0)
      if p_weights.sum()==0: continue
      p_prob=p_weights/p_weights.sum()
      q_weights=1.0/(1.0+W)
      q_prob=q_weights/q_weights.sum()
      p=int(rng.choice(n, p=p_prob)); q=int(rng.choice(n, p=q_prob))
      if p==q: continue
      delta=0.1*best[p]
      for scale in (1,0.5,0.2,0.05):
         b=best.copy(); b[p]-=delta*scale; b[q]+=delta*scale
         b=np.clip(b,0,None); b=normalize(b)
         sc=fast_score(b)
         if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
             best=b; best_sc=sc; break
   return best,best_sc
```
But using `np.random.choice` with p for n large many times could be slow. Limit n<200 for coordinate. For larger, use simpler random index.

```
      width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//10)))
      start=...
      b[start:stop]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,base_scale))
      if rng.random()<0.3: flip? average
      b=normalise
      sc=fast_score(b)
      if sc<best_sc: best=b; best_sc=sc; base_scale=min(0.2,base_scale*1.1)
      else: if it>20 base_scale=max(0.01, base_scale*0.95)
```

```
def lp_direction(a, rng=None, eps=1e-6):
   n=len(a); M=fast_score? Need max conv only: c=np.convolve(a,a); M=c.max()
   A=build_convolution_matrix(a)
   # Scale constraints to better conditioning: A *= 1/M? b_ub=1. Since b can large, maybe okay.
   A = A / max(M, tiny)
   b_ub = np.ones(2n-1)
   cvec = -np.ones(n)
   if rng is not None: cvec += eps*rng.normal(0,1,n)
   res=linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=n*(0,None), method='highs', options={'disp':False})
   if res.success: b=clip(res.x); s=b.sum(); if s>1e-12 return b/s
   return None
```
Scaling A by M makes constraints ∑(a/M)g ≤1. a/M ~n, variables maybe? For uniform a=1/n,M=1/n, A entries 1, b_ub=1. Good. For sparse a_i=1 (M=1), A entries up to1. Good.

```
def lp_step(a, b, max_sc, rng):
   candidates_t=[0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]
   for t in candidates_t:
     c=(1-t)*a+t*b; c=normalize; sc=fast_score(c); if sc<max_sc: return c,sc
   return None,None
```

```
rng=default_rng(seed)
iters_since_improve=0
while time < limit:
   improved=False
   for _ in range(4):
      b=lp_direction(a, rng)
      if b is None: break
      cand,sc=lp_step(a,b,best_sc,rng)
      if cand is not None:
          a=cand; best=cand; best_sc=sc; improved=True; break
   if improved: iters=0 else iters++
   # perturb if stuck
   if iters>=2:
      cand=random smooth perturb; if improves update
      iters=0
   # maybe re-normalize
   if time elapsed break
return best,best_sc
```

Parameterize a = exp(θ_i)/∑exp(θ_j) (positive simplex). Then objective smooth. Use Nelder-Mead on θ (n dims) or L-BFGS. This avoids zeros, but optimal may have zeros? Maybe not for target? Could allow zeros by clipping. But nonnegative with zeros possible in sparse sequences; for target maybe positive. Use softmax for smoothness. However values all positive; endpoints can be very small.

For n up to maybe 12, run L-BFGS on θ with exact objective. Objective as M of convolution of softmax. This is nonconvex but differentiable except max. We can use softmax approx for gradient. Or use `scipy.optimize.minimize` on θ with method='Nelder-Mead' no gradient.

But LP may handle zeros.

Candidate evaluation top-level:
```
def score_sequence(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   if a.size==0 or not np.isfinite(a).all(): return np.inf
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<0.01: return np.inf
   # for internal optimize normalize? The true score with clipping and no normalization:
   c=np.convolve(a,a)
   return 2*a.size*np.max(c)/(s*s)
```

```
def consider(a, best, best_score):
    if a is None: return best,best_score
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0 or not np.isfinite(a).all(): return best,best_score
    a_clip=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a_clip.sum()

    sc=score_sequence(a_clip)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
        norm=normalize_nonneg(a_clip)
        if norm is None: norm=a_clip/s
        return norm, sc
    return best,best_score
```

Top-level helpers:

No lambdas.

`normalize_nonneg`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return a / s
```

`score_sequence`:
```
def score_sequence(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   if a.size==0: return np.inf
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return np.inf
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=float(np.sum(a))
   if s < 0.01: return np.inf
   c=np.convolve(a,a)
   m=float(np.max(c))
   if not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf
   return float(2.0*len(a)*m/(s*s))
```
For normalized sum1, no cap effect.

`consider`:
```
def consider(a, best, best_score):
    a = np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return best,best_score
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return best,best_score
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000.0)
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01: return best,best_score
    sc=score_sequence(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
       nrm = a/s
       return nrm, sc
    return best,best_score
```
If a has values >1000, normalizing after clip may have sum different; nrm = a_clip/s_clip. Code should clip first.

`beta_sequence` optional center=True:
```
def beta_sequence(n, pa, pb):
   n=max(2,int(n))
   x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
   # avoid under/overflow: logx=np.log(x), log1=np.log1p(-x)
   a=np.exp(pa*np.log(x)+pb*np.log1p(-x))
   a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=0: return None
   return a/s
```
Need if pa,pb large cause overflow; log values okay; exp maybe overflow if pa* -inf? x finite >0, <1, logs finite; pa up to e.g.5, log ~ -13, product -65 ok. If pa=-5, product 65 exp large but maybe 1e28, finite; can handle with nan_to_num and normalize.

`random_smooth_sequence(n, rng, kernel_size=5)`:
```
def random_smooth_sequence(n, rng, kernel_size=None):
    n=max(2,int(n))
    if kernel_size is None: kernel_size=max(3, n//20)
    base=rng.exponential(1.0, size=n)
    base[0:]=... maybe add rng.uniform
    kern=np.ones(kernel_size)/kernel_size
    b=np.convolve(base, kern, mode='same')
    if rng.random()<0.25: b=0.5*(b+b[::-1])
    b=np.clip(b,0,1000)
    s=b.sum()
    if s<=1e-15: return None
    return b/s
```

`block_sequence(n, ratio)`: first n//2 ones then twos.
```
def two_block_sequence(n):
    n=max(4,int(n)); a=np.ones(n); a[:n//2]=1; a[n//2:]=2; return normalize_nonneg(a)
```

`def ramp_sequence(n, reverse=False, p=1.0)`:
```
x=np.arange(1,n+1, float)
if p !=1: x=x**p
if reverse: x=x[::-1]
return normalize_nonneg(x)
```

Local improve functions.
`perturb_improve(a, rng, max_iters, initial_scale=0.1)`:
```
def perturb_improve(a, rng, max_iters, deadline=None, initial_scale=0.1):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a); best=a; best_sc=score_sequence(a); scale=initial_scale; stall=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if deadline is not None and time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       n=len(a)
       if n < 64:
          width=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//10 + 1)))
          start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
       else:
          width=int(rng.integers(max(1, n//200), max(2, n//20)))
          start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
       stop=min(n, start+width)
       b=best.copy()
       b[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale)))
       if rng.random() < 0.25:
          b = 0.5*(b + b[::-1])
       b=normalize_nonneg(b)
       if b is None: continue
       sc=score_sequence(b)
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          best=b; best_sc=sc; stall=0; scale=min(0.25, scale*1.15)
       else:
          stall += 1
          if stall > 25:
             scale=max(0.01, scale*0.7)
             stall=0
    return best,best_sc
```
Note symmetric average changes sum but normalize.

`coord_improve(a, rng, max_iters, deadline=None)` top-level. To be efficient, only if n<=200.
```
def coord_improve(a, rng, max_iters, deadline=None):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a); best=a; best_sc=score_sequence(a); n=len(a)
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if deadline and time.monotonic()>deadline: break
      c=np.convolve(best,best); M=c.max()
      if not finite: break
      idxs=np.where(c >= M*(1.0-1e-3))[0]
      k=int(idxs[int(rng.integers(len(idxs)))])
      W=np.zeros(n)
      lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
      # assign W[lo:hi+1] = best[k - np.arange(lo,hi+1)]
      W[lo:hi+1] = best[k - np.arange(lo,hi+1)]
      contrib=np.maximum(best*W,0)
      total=contrib.sum()
      if total <= 1e-15: continue
      # generate few random transfers
      for _ in range(8):
         p=int(rng.integers(n))
         # choose p weighted if possible: use rng.choice with p weights? To avoid p array length maybe okay
         # Use p=CDF: p_idx = int(rng.random()*total)?? Simpler p = rng.choice(n, p=contrib/total)
         # q = rng.choice(n, p=(1/(1+W))/...) but can be slow. Use p weighted, q uniform/random low W
         # We'll just do p weighted, q uniformly.
         p=int(rng.choice(n, p=contrib/total))
         q=int(rng.integers(n))
         if p==q: continue
         for frac in (0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02):
             delta=frac*best[p]
             b=best.copy(); b[p]-=delta; b[q]+=delta
             b=normalize_nonneg(b)
             if b is None: continue
             sc=score_sequence(b)
             if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best=b; best_sc=sc
             break? Need if no accept at larger frac, try smaller.
```

```
for frac in (0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03):
   ... if accept break
```

`lp_direction` top-level:
```
def lp_direction(a, rng=None, eps=1e-5):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None: return None
    n=len(a)
    if n < 2: return None
    if not HAS_LINPROG: return None
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 1e-15: return None
    # Build dense A
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    # To speed, fill with loop
    for t in range(2*n-1):
       lo=max(0,t-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,t)
       if lo <= hi:
          idx=np.arange(lo,hi+1)
          A[t, idx] = a[t - idx]
    A = A / M
    b_ub = np.ones(2*n-1, dtype=np.float64)
    cvec = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if rng is not None and eps>0:
       cvec = cvec + eps * (rng.random(n)-0.5)
    try:
       res=linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=n*((0.0, None)), method='highs', options={'disp':0, 'presolve': True})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or not getattr(res,'success',False):
       return None
    b=np.asarray(res.x).ravel()
    b=np.clip(b,0,1000)
    s=float(np.sum(b))
    if s <= 1e-12:
       return None
    return b/s
```
Note `bounds=n*((0.0, None))` for linprog accepted.

`lp_step` top-level:
```
def lp_step(a,b,best_sc):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a); b=normalize_nonneg(b)
    if a is None or b is None: return None,np.inf
    for t in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0):
       c=(1-t)*a + t*b
       c=normalize_nonneg(c)
       if c is None: continue
       sc=score_sequence(c)
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          return c, sc
    # if direction alone better (t=1 included)
    return None,np.inf
```

`lp_optimize` top-level:
```
def lp_optimize(a0, seed, deadline, max_iter=500):
    a=normalize_nonneg(a0)
    if a is None: return None,np.inf
    best=a; best_sc=score_sequence(a)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    stuck=0
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       improved=False
       # Try different random eps to get diverse LP directions
       for eps in (0.0, 1e-5, 1e-4, 1e-3):
          b=lp_direction(a, rng, eps if eps>0 else 0.0)
          if b is None: continue
          cand,sc=lp_step(a,b,best_sc)
          if cand is not None:
             a=cand; best=cand; best_sc=sc; improved=True; break
          # consider b alone
          nb,sb=consider(b,best,best_sc)? Actually consider expects a and best,bestscore; but b normalized. Use score.
          if sc? lp_step already included t=1. If not better no.
       if improved:
          stuck=0
       else:
          stuck +=1
          if stuck >= 3:
             # random perturb to escape
             cand,sc=perturb_improve(a, rng, 30, deadline, initial_scale=0.15)
             if sc < best_sc:
                 best=cand; best_sc=sc; a=cand; stuck=0
             else:
                 # if too many stuck break
                 if stuck > 12: break
    return best,best_sc
```
But `perturb_improve` may take time; limit.

```python
import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    _HAS_LINPROG = True
except Exception:
    _HAS_LINPROG = False


def normalize_nonneg(a):
    """Clip to the evaluator's domain and normalize to unit sum."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return a / s


def score_sequence(a):
    """Exact same objective as evaluate_sequence, but on a numpy array."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return np.inf
    c = np.convolve(a, a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * m / (s * s))


def consider(a, best, best_score):
    """Update best if a gives a better exact score."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return best, best_score
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return best, best_score
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return best, best_score
    sc = score_sequence(a)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
        return a / s, sc
    return best, best_score


def beta_sequence(n, pa, pb):
    """Positive beta-shaped sequence x^pa * (1-x)^pb on [0, 1]."""
    n = max(2, int(n))
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    lx = np.log(x)
    rx = np.log1p(-x)
    a = np.exp(float(pa) * lx + float(pb) * rx)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return a / s


def two_peak_sequence(n, p, w):
    """Two symmetric Gaussian-like bumps at p and 1-p."""
    n = max(2, int(n))
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    w = max(1e-3, float(w))
    p = float(np.clip(p, 0.01, 0.49))
    a = np.exp(-(((x - p) / w) ** 2)) + np.exp(-(((x - (1.0 - p)) / w) ** 2))
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return a / s


def ramp_sequence(n, reverse=False, p=1.0):
    """Monotone ramp (i+1)^p, optionally reversed."""
    n = max(2, int(n))
    x = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    if p != 1.0:
        x = np.power(x, float(p))
    if reverse:
        x = x[::-1]
    return normalize_nonneg(x)


def two_block_sequence(n):
    """Two adjacent constant blocks: 1s then 2s."""
    n = max(4, int(n))
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:n // 2] = 1.0
    a[n // 2:] = 2.0
    return normalize_nonneg(a)


def random_smooth_sequence(n, rng, kernel_size=None):
    """Random smoothed nonnegative sequence."""
    n = max(2, int(n))
    if kernel_size is None:
        kernel_size = max(3, n // 20)
    base = rng.exponential(1.0, size=n)
    base += 1e-3
    kern = np.ones(int(kernel_size), dtype=np.float64)
    kern /= kern.sum()
    b = np.convolve(base, kern, mode="same")
    if rng.random() < 0.25:
        b = 0.5 * (b + b[::-1])
    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(b))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return b / s


def initial_sweeps(best, best_score, deadline):
    """Sweep cheap parametric families to get a strong first guess."""
    # Beta shapes. Include mild endpoint singularities.
    ns = [16, 32, 64, 128, 256]
    grid = np.arange(-0.5, 3.0 + 1e-9, 0.25)
    for n in ns:
        for pa in grid:
            for pb in grid:
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    return best, best_score
                seq = beta_sequence(n, float(pa), float(pb))
                best, best_score = consider(seq, best, best_score)

    # Smoothed two-bump shapes, a bridge between interval blocks and delta pairs.
    for n in [32, 64, 128, 256]:
        for p in [0.05, 0.08, 0.12, 0.16, 0.20]:
            for w in [0.04, 0.07, 0.10, 0.14, 0.20, 0.28, 0.38]:
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    return best, best_score
                seq = two_peak_sequence(n, p, w)
                best, best_score = consider(seq, best, best_score)

    return best, best_score


def perturb_improve(a, rng, max_iters, deadline=None, initial_scale=0.15):
    """Random multiplicative-block hill climbing with normalization."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, np.inf
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_sequence(a)
    scale = max(0.01, float(initial_scale))
    stall = 0
    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        n = len(best)
        if n < 64:
            width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 10 + 1)))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        else:
            width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 200), max(2, n // 20)))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)
        cand = best.copy()
        cand[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale)))
        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            cand = 0.5 * (cand + cand[::-1])
        cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
        if cand is None:
            continue
        sc = score_sequence(cand)
        if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best = cand
            best_sc = sc
            stall = 0
            scale = min(0.30, scale * 1.15)
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 25:
                scale = max(0.01, scale * 0.70)
                stall = 0
    return best, best_sc


def coord_improve(a, rng, max_iters, deadline=None):
    """Move mass away from currently maximal lag contributions."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, np.inf
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_sequence(a)
    n = len(best)
    if n > 300:
        return best, best_sc
    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        if deadline is not None and time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        c = np.convolve(best, best)
        m = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0:
            break
        idxs = np.where(c >= m * (1.0 - 1e-3))[0]
        k = int(idxs[int(rng.integers(len(idxs)))])
        w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if lo <= hi:
            w[lo:hi + 1] = best[k - np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)]
        contrib = np.maximum(best * w, 0.0)
        total = float(contrib.sum())
        if total <= 1e-15:
            continue
        pvec = contrib / total
        pvec = np.nan_to_num(pvec, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        psum = float(pvec.sum())
        if psum <= 1e-15:
            continue
        pvec = pvec / psum
        accepted = False
        for _m in range(10):
            p = int(rng.choice(n, p=pvec))
            q = int(rng.integers(n))
            if p == q or best[p] <= 0:
                continue
            for frac in (0.25, 0.125, 0.0625, 0.03):
                delta = frac * float(best[p])
                cand = best.copy()
                cand[p] -= delta
                cand[q] += delta
                cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
                if cand is None:
                    continue
                sc = score_sequence(cand)
                if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best = cand
                    best_sc = sc
                    accepted = True
                    break
            if accepted:
                break
    return best, best_sc


def lp_direction(a, rng=None, eps=1e-5):
    """Solve the linear LP direction from the hint: max sum b s.t. a*b <= M."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None:
        return None
    if not _HAS_LINPROG:
        return None
    n = len(a)
    if n < 2 or n > 400:
        return None
    c = np.convolve(a, a)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 1e-15:
        return None

    A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for t in range(2 * n - 1):
        lo = max(0, t - (n - 1))
        hi = min(n - 1, t)
        if lo <= hi:
            idx = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
            A[t, idx] = a[t - idx]

    A = A / m
    b_ub = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cvec = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if rng is not None and eps > 0:
        cvec = cvec + float(eps) * (rng.random(n) - 0.5)

    try:
        res = linprog(
            cvec,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=[(0.0, None)] * n,
            method="highs",
            options={"disp": False}
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
        return None
    x = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if x is None:
        return None
    b = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    b = np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(b))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return b / s


def lp_step(a, b, best_sc):
    """Line-search between current a and LP direction b."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    b = normalize_nonneg(b)
    if a is None or b is None:
        return None, np.inf
    for t in (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.50, 0.80, 1.00):
        cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * b
        cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
        if cand is None:
            continue
        sc = score_sequence(cand)
        if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
            return cand, sc
    return None, np.inf


def lp_optimize(a0, seed, deadline, max_iter=350):
    """Iterate the LP direction and exact line search."""
    a = normalize_nonneg(a0)
    if a is None:
        return None, np.inf
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_sequence(a)
    if len(best) > 380:
        return best, best_sc

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    stuck = 0
    for _ in range(int(max_iter)):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        improved = False
        for eps in (0.0, 1e-5, 1e-4, 1e-3):
            b = lp_direction(a, rng, eps if eps > 0 else 0.0)
            if b is None:
                continue
            cand, sc = lp_step(a, b, best_sc)
            if cand is not None:
                a = cand
                best = cand
                best_sc = sc
                improved = True
                break
        if improved:
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck >= 3:
                cand, sc = perturb_improve(a, rng, 40, deadline, initial_scale=0.18)
                if cand is not None and sc < best_sc:
                    best = cand
                    best_sc = sc
                    a = cand
                    stuck = 0
                elif stuck > 12:
                    break
    return best, best_sc


def make_key_starts(n, rng):
    """A small diverse portfolio for LP/local refinement at length n."""
    out = []
    a = normalize_nonneg(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    if a is not None:
        out.append(a)
    out.append(two_block_sequence(n))
    out.append(ramp_sequence(n))
    out.append(ramp_sequence(n, reverse=True))
    out.append(beta_sequence(n, 0.0, 0.0))
    out.append(beta_sequence(n, 0.5, 1.0))
    out.append(beta_sequence(n, 1.0, 0.5))
    out.append(beta_sequence(n, -0.25, 1.0))
    out.append(two_peak_sequence(n, 0.15, 0.20))
    out.append(random_smooth_sequence(n, rng))
    return [x for x in out if x is not None]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Hybrid search for a nonnegative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a))^2.

    Strategy:
      1. Start from available baseline and cheap parametric families.
      2. Use several local optimizers:
         - normalized block perturbations
         - mass moves guided by active maximal lag
         - LP-direction iteration inspired by the hint
      3. Keep the exact best sequence found under the imposed time budget.
    """
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 6.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best = None
    best_score = np.inf

    # Use the provided baseline if present.
    try:
        base = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        base = np.clip(base, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(np.sum(base))
        if s > 0.01:
            best, best_score = consider(base, best, best_score)
            if best is not None:
                best = best.copy()
    except Exception:
        pass

    if best is None:
        best = normalize_nonneg(np.ones(16, dtype=np.float64))
        best_score = score_sequence(best)

    # Cheap global sweeps.
    sweep_deadline = min(deadline, start_time + 420.0)
    best, best_score = initial_sweeps(best, best_score, sweep_deadline)
    print("after initial sweeps", best_score, "len", len(best))

    # LP and local refinement over many lengths/starts.
    lp_ns = [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 16, 22, 32, 48, 80, 128]
    lp_deadline = min(deadline, start_time + (deadline - start_time) * 0.70)

    # Always include a copy of the current best if LP-feasible.
    if len(best) <= 380:
        b, sc = lp_optimize(best, int(seed), min(lp_deadline, time.monotonic() + 120.0), max_iter=180)
        best, best_score = consider(b, best, best_score)
        print("after LP on current best", best_score, "len", len(best))

    starts = []
    for n in lp_ns:
        if time.monotonic() > lp_deadline:
            break
        for a0 in make_key_starts(n, rng):
            best, best_score = consider(a0, best, best_score)
            starts.append(a0)

    # Allocate remaining LP time across starts.
    for i, a0 in enumerate(starts):
        if time.monotonic() >= lp_deadline:
            break
        remaining = lp_deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 4.0:
            break
        nleft = max(1, len(starts) - i)
        slice_time = min(70.0, remaining / float(nleft))
        slice_time = max(3.0, slice_time)
        b, sc = lp_optimize(a0, int(seed + 1000 + i), time.monotonic() + slice_time, max_iter=160)
        best, best_score = consider(b, best, best_score)
        if i % 12 == 0:
            print("LP progress", i, "/", len(starts), "score", best_score, "len", len(best))

    # Final exact polishing using remaining time.
    polish_deadline = deadline - 8.0
    b, sc = perturb_improve(best, rng, 20000, polish_deadline, initial_scale=0.12)
    best, best_score = consider(b, best, best_score)

    if len(best) <= 300:
        b, sc = coord_improve(best, rng, 8000, polish_deadline)
        best, best_score = consider(b, best, best_score)

    if len(best) <= 380:
        b, sc = lp_optimize(best, int(seed + 777), time.monotonic() + min(300.0, max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 4.0)), max_iter=250)
        best, best_score = consider(b, best, best_score)

    # One last brief random polish.
    b, sc = perturb_improve(best, rng, 5000, deadline - 2.0, initial_scale=0.08)
    best, best_score = consider(b, best, best_score)

    if best is None:
        best = normalize_nonneg(np.ones(10, dtype=np.float64))

    final_list = [float(x) for x in best]

    # Validate against the authoritative evaluator if available.
    try:
        true_score = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        if not np.isfinite(true_score):
            final_list = [float(x) for x in normalize_nonneg(np.ones(10, dtype=np.float64))]
        else:
            best_score = float(true_score)
    except Exception:
        best_score = score_sequence(np.asarray(final_list, dtype=np.float64))

    print("final internal score", best_score, "len", len(final_list))
    return final_list
```
